Ezekiel 38:3
and say to him, ‘Thus says the Lord, the Lord: Behold, I am against you, Gog, ruler of Rhos, Mosoch and Thobel,
  Ezekiel 39:1 — close repeated declaration of opposition to Gog: Both oracles declare that God is against Gog and repeat the same description involving Rhos, Mosoch and Thobel. This is a textual correspondence within the Gog oracles, without an external identification of the ruler.

Ezekiel 38:4

Ezekiel 38:5

  Ezekiel 27:10 — shared foreign contingents and defensive equipment: Gog's force includes Persians and Libyans with helmets and small shields.   Jeremiah 26:9 — shared Ethiopian and Libyan warriors in different forces: Ezekiel includes Ethiopians and Libyans among Gog's equipped troops. Ezekiel 38:8

Ezekiel 38:10

Micah condemns people who plan evil on their beds and then covet fields and plunder people, houses and inheritances; the scale and actors differ.

Ezekiel 38:11
and say, “I will go up against an abandoned land; I will come upon those resting quietly and dwelling in peace, all living in a land with no wall or bars, and they have no doors,”
  Judges 18:7-10 — related aggressive designs on a quietly settled people: Gog proposes to attack those resting quietly and living in peace. The Danite scouts similarly describe Laish's people living in confidence and urge an attack; Judges does not supply Ezekiel's explicit absence of walls, bars and doors.

Ezekiel 38:12

  Ezekiel 36:33-35 — restored desolate land becomes the object of the invader's plunder: Gog aims at a formerly desolate land now inhabited and a people accumulating possessions. The earlier promise describes precisely such a transition from devastated places to inhabited cities and worked land; it does not explain the source's earth's-navel phrase.

Ezekiel 38:13

  Ezekiel 27:12 — shared Carthaginian merchants associated with silver and gold: The source names Carthaginian merchants among those asking whether Gog seeks silver and gold as plunder. The Tyre trading list also names Carthaginians and their silver and gold, in commerce rather than the source's inquiry about spoils.

Ezekiel 38:14

  Judges 18:9-10 — related stirring of an aggressor against a confident population: The question asks whether Gog will be roused when Israel is settled in peace. The scouts urge their brothers to arise and invade a land whose people dwell in confidence; the comparison concerns aggressive response to security, not the identity of the peoples.

Ezekiel 38:15
You will come from your place in the farthest north, with many nations beside you, all horse riders, a great assembly and a mighty force,
  Ezekiel 39:2 — close repeated northern approach of Gog: The source brings Gog and many nations from the farthest north. The following oracle likewise says God will bring him from the farthest north onto Israel's mountains; it does not repeat the cavalry inventory.

Ezekiel 38:16
and come up against My people Israel like a cloud covering the land. It will be at the end of days; I will bring you against My land so that all the nations may know Me when I am sanctified in you before them.
  Ezekiel 36:23 — shared public sanctification before the nations in different settings: God brings Gog against His land so nations know Him when He is sanctified before them. The restoration oracle likewise promises public sanctification and the nations' knowledge, but there the immediate setting is His action in Israel rather than the invading Gog.

Ezekiel 38:18

  Ezekiel 39:8 — parallel day announcements within the Gog oracles: The source announces the day Gog comes and God's wrath rises. The following Gog oracle declares arrival and future knowledge, calling this the day on which God spoke. The link concerns their related day declarations, without changing on which to of which or claiming an observed historical fulfillment.

Ezekiel 38:19
and My jealousy. In the fire of My anger I have spoken: Surely on that day there will be a great shaking upon Israel’s land,
  Nahum 1:5-6 — parallel shaking of the land before divine anger: Ezekiel connects jealousy and fiery anger with a great shaking of Israel's land. Nahum likewise places shaking mountains and earth beside God's furious wrath and crushed rocks; its geographical scope differs.

Ezekiel 38:20
and the sea’s fish, the sky’s birds, the plain’s beasts, every creeping thing upon the earth and every human upon the earth’s face will shake before the Lord. The mountains will split, the ravines will fall, and every wall will fall to the ground.
  Nahum 1:5-6 — close terrestrial convulsion before the Lord: Ezekiel describes creatures and humans shaking, with mountains splitting and ravines and walls falling. Nahum similarly describes mountains and hills shaking, the earth recoiling and rocks crushed before God's anger, without Ezekiel's full creature catalogue.
  Haggai 2:6-7 — related comprehensive shaking involving earth and nations: Ezekiel's shaking reaches sea creatures, birds, earthly creatures and every human, with collapsing terrain. Haggai promises shaking of heaven, earth, sea, dry land and all nations; its setting concerns the house's coming glory rather than Gog's attack.

Ezekiel 38:21

  Judges 7:22 — close judgment through swords turned against companions: Ezekiel announces that a man's sword will be against his brother. In Gideon's battle the Lord sets each man's sword against his neighbor throughout the enemy camp; the target supplies a narrated instance of this form of defeat.

Ezekiel 38:22
I will judge him with death, blood, flooding rain and hailstones. I will rain fire and sulfur upon him, all those with him and the many nations with him.
  Genesis 19:24 — close divine rain of fire and sulfur: God threatens to rain fire and sulfur on Gog and his accompanying nations. Genesis records the Lord raining sulfur and fire upon Sodom and Gomorrah; the judged peoples and settings differ.
  Joshua 10:11 — related destruction of an enemy force by divinely sent hailstones: Ezekiel includes hailstones among judgments against Gog's force. Joshua records the Lord hurling hailstones from heaven on a fleeing enemy force, causing more deaths than Israel's sword; Ezekiel supplies no such numerical comparison.
  Exodus 9:23-26 — related divine judgment combining hail and fire: God announces judgment on Gog and the assembled nations with hailstones, fire and sulfur. In Exodus, the Lord rains hail upon Egypt with fire amid it, striking people, livestock and vegetation while sparing Goshen. Hail and fire are the particular shared combination; the target does not mention sulfur or identify the two judgments as one event.

Ezekiel 38:23
I will be magnified, sanctified, glorified and made known before many nations, and they will know that I am the Lord.’”
  Ezekiel 36:23 — close shared sanctification and recognition before nations: Both declarations join God's being sanctified publicly with the nations knowing that He is the Lord. The source adds magnification and glorification after the Gog judgment; the earlier oracle emphasizes vindicating the profaned name.

Ezekiel 39:1
“And you, son of man, prophesy against Gog and say, ‘Thus says the Lord: Behold, I am against you, Gog, ruler of Rhos, Mosoch and Thobel,
  Ezekiel 38:2-3 — repeated prophetic commission and opposition to Gog: The source repeats the command to prophesy against Gog and the declaration that God is against him. The earlier opening supplies the same Rhos, Mosoch and Thobel description and additionally mentions Magog's land.

Ezekiel 39:2

  Ezekiel 38:15-16 — repeated divine bringing of Gog from the north against Israel: Both oracles describe Gog coming from the farthest north against Israel under God's declared action. The earlier passage adds the great mounted force and the purpose of making God known to nations.

Ezekiel 39:3
I will destroy your bow from your left hand and your arrows from your right hand, and strike you down
  Psalms 45:10 — related divine destruction of a bow and weapons: God destroys Gog's bow and arrows. The psalm likewise describes God shattering a bow and breaking a weapon while ending wars; it additionally describes burning shields rather than the source's arrows in the right hand.

Ezekiel 39:4
upon Israel’s mountains. You and all those around you will fall, and the nations with you will be given to multitudes of birds. I have given you to every flying creature and all the plain’s beasts to be eaten.

Ezekiel 39:5

Ezekiel 39:6

Ezekiel 39:7

Ezekiel 39:8
Behold, it has arrived, and you will know that it will be, says the Lord, the Lord. This is the day on which I spoke.

Ezekiel 39:9

Ezekiel 39:10
They certainly will not take wood from the plain or cut it from the woods; they will burn the weapons with fire. They will plunder those who plundered them and strip those who stripped them, says the Lord.
  Obadiah 1:17 — related reversal upon former dispossessors: Ezekiel promises that the people will plunder those who plundered them and strip those who stripped them. Ezekiel 39:11
On that day I will give Gog a notable place, a memorial tomb in Israel, the mass grave of those who came against it, beside the sea. They will build around the valley’s mouth and bury Gog and all his multitude there. It will be called Gai, Gog’s Mass Grave.
  Jeremiah 7:32 — related renaming of a mass-burial place after judgment: Ezekiel names the valley of Gog's buried multitude Gai, Gog's Mass Grave. Jeremiah likewise announces a changed place-name, Ravine of the Slain, associated with burial after judgment. The names, victims and locations are distinct; this does not identify Gai with Topheth.

Ezekiel 39:12

  Deuteronomy 21:22-23 — related burial intended to prevent defilement of the land: Ezekiel says Israel buries the multitude to cleanse the land. Deuteronomy requires burial of an executed person's displayed body so the given land is not defiled; its same-day requirement concerns a different case from Ezekiel's seven-month task.

Ezekiel 39:14
They will continually designate men to pass through the land and bury those left upon the land’s surface, to cleanse it after the seven months, and they will search.
  Numbers 19:16 — ritual-impurity context for clearing exposed human remains: Ezekiel's oracle describes people designating buriers for remains left on the land's surface to cleanse it. Numbers treats contact with a slain person, corpse, human bone or tomb on the open plain as uncleanness; it does not prescribe Ezekiel's search teams or seven-month schedule.

Ezekiel 39:15

  Numbers 19:16 — particular uncleanness counterpart for a discovered human bone: Ezekiel's oracle directs a passerby who finds a human bone to mark it for later burial. Numbers explicitly includes touching a human bone on the open plain among causes of uncleanness. The burial marker belongs to Ezekiel's cleanup procedure, not to the selected law.

Ezekiel 39:16
Indeed, the city’s name will be Mass Grave, and the land will be cleansed.
  Jeremiah 7:32 — related place-name derived from collective death and burial: The source names a city Mass Grave and connects the setting with a cleansed land. Jeremiah renames a burial ravine Ravine of the Slain. This compares commemorative naming after judgment while keeping city and ravine, names and locations distinct.

Ezekiel 39:17
And you, son of man, say, Thus says the Lord: Say to every flying bird and all the plain’s beasts, Gather and come! Gather from all those around to My sacrifice that I have sacrificed for you, a great sacrifice upon Israel’s mountains. You will eat flesh and drink blood.
  Jeremiah 12:9 — close summons for wild creatures to assemble and eat the judged victim: Ezekiel commands birds and beasts to gather to God's sacrificial feast on the defeated force. Jeremiah likewise commands gathering the field's wild beasts to eat the abandoned inheritance; its victim is God's inheritance rather than Gog's force.

Ezekiel 39:18
You will eat giants’ flesh and drink the blood of the land’s rulers: rams, calves and male goats, and the calves all fattened.
  Isaiah 34:6-7 — related sacrificial animal imagery for a devastating judgment: Ezekiel depicts rulers' blood and giants' flesh through rams, calves and male goats. Isaiah describes God's sword filled with blood and fat of lambs, male goats and rams in a sacrifice upon Idumea, with stout ones, rams and bulls falling. The peoples and animal lists differ.

Ezekiel 39:19

  Isaiah 34:6-7 — close sacrificial imagery of fat, blood and drunken fullness: Ezekiel's summoned creatures eat fat to fullness and drink blood to drunkenness from God's sacrifice. Isaiah's sacrifice likewise fills the sword and earth with blood and fat and describes the earth as drunk with blood; the recipients of fullness differ.

Ezekiel 39:20

  Jeremiah 26:9-10 — related portrayal of an equipped force consumed in a divine sacrificial feast: Ezekiel's table fills the summoned creatures with horse, rider, giant and warrior. Jeremiah names horses and foreign warriors before depicting God's sword eating to fullness and drunkenness at His sacrifice. The eaters and defeated forces differ, and Jeremiah does not use Ezekiel's table wording.

Ezekiel 39:21
I will set My glory among you, and all the nations will see My judgment that I executed and My hand that I brought upon them.
  Ezekiel 38:23 — repeated public glorification after judgment on Gog: Ezekiel promises glory among the addressed audience and the nations' sight of His executed judgment. The preceding Gog oracle likewise joins His glorification and manifestation before many nations with their recognition of Him.

Ezekiel 39:23
All the nations will know that Israel’s house was taken captive because of their sins, because they acted faithlessly toward Me. I turned My face from them and handed them into their enemies’ hands, and all fell by the sword.
  Deuteronomy 29:23-27 — close explanation to nations of Israel's exile through covenant wrongdoing: Ezekiel says nations will know Israel was captive because of faithlessness. Deuteronomy likewise has nations ask why the land suffered, and answers that abandoning the covenant and serving other gods led to removal into another land.

Ezekiel 39:24
I dealt with them according to their uncleannesses and lawless acts, and turned My face from them.
  Deuteronomy 31:17-18 — close turning of God's face because of the people's wrongdoing: Ezekiel attributes God's turned face to the people's uncleannesses and lawless acts. Deuteronomy likewise says God turns His face because of the people's wickedness and foreign gods, with ensuing afflictions.

Ezekiel 39:25

  Ezekiel 46:18 — related protection of Israel's inherited land from rulers' oppression: The ruler's possession is followed by a promise that Israel's rulers will no longer oppress the people and that the tribes will inherit land. Ezekiel 46 specifies that a ruler must give his sons land from his own possession rather than take the people's inheritance and scatter them from it.

Ezekiel 45:9

  Jeremiah 22:1-3 — parallel royal duty to practice justice and remove oppression: Israel's rulers must remove injustice, suffering and oppression and practice judgment and righteousness. Leviticus likewise forbids injustice in measures, weights and scales and requires just scales, weights and a chous; the shared ethical standard does not identify the differently named capacity units.

Ezekiel 45:11

  Deuteronomy 25:14-15 — related consistency of capacity standards: Ezekiel makes the measure and choenix equal, each a tenth of the gomor. Deuteronomy forbids differing large and small household measures and requires a true, just measure; it supports consistent measurement without supplying Ezekiel's units or conversion.

Ezekiel 45:12

  Numbers 18:16 — shared twenty-obol weight standard: Ezekiel states that the standard weight is twenty obols before discussing shekels and a fifty-shekel mina. Numbers likewise defines the holy shekel as twenty obols in its redemption valuation, without supplying Ezekiel's mina calculation.

Joshua's Naphtali allotment likewise joins Asher, there toward the sea and with Zebulun to the south; the shared tribal adjacency does not imply identical directional plans.

Ezekiel 48:4
From Naphtali’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Manasseh, one portion.
  Joshua 17:1-2 — related territorial allotment for Manasseh: Manasseh receives a portion following Naphtali's boundaries. Joshua likewise sets out Manasseh's allotments, identifying Joseph's firstborn and distinguishing Machir's Gilead-and-Bashan inheritance from the other family divisions rather than presenting Ezekiel's east-to-sea strip.

Ezekiel 48:5
From Manasseh’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Ephraim, one portion.
  Joshua 16:8-9 — related Ephraim allotment in relation to Manasseh: Ephraim's portion extends from Manasseh's boundaries. Joshua likewise describes Ephraim's inheritance and cities set apart for Ephraim amid Manasseh's inheritance, retaining the difference between a boundary adjacency and cities within another tribal allotment.

Ezekiel 48:6
From Ephraim’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Reuben, one portion.
  Joshua 13:15-23 — related territorial allotment for Reuben: Reuben receives an east-to-sea portion from Ephraim's boundaries. Joshua likewise recounts Reuben's inheritance from Moses, naming the Arnon region and Jordan boundary; it supplies a different territorial description rather than the same sequence of neighboring strips.

Ezekiel 48:7
From Reuben’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Judah, one portion.
  Joshua 15:1-4 — related territorial boundaries for Judah: Judah receives a portion from Reuben's boundaries. Joshua likewise delineates Judah's territory, beginning from Idumea and the wilderness of Zin on the southwest and ending that boundary at the sea, without reproducing Ezekiel's neighboring-tribe arrangement.

Ezekiel 48:8
From Judah’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts, will be the first portion set apart, twenty-five thousand in breadth, with a length like one of the portions from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts. The holy place will be in their midst.
  Ezekiel 45:3 — related sanctuary within a reserved holy land portion: The first portion adjoining Judah has the holy place in its midst. Ezekiel 45 likewise reserves a measured area containing the sanctuary, there stating a twenty-five-thousand by ten-thousand plot; the counterpart concerns the sanctuary-bearing reservation rather than identical outer dimensions.

Ezekiel 48:9
The first portion they set apart for the Lord will be twenty-five thousand long and twenty-five thousand broad.
  Ezekiel 45:1 — related measured first portion set apart for the Lord: The Lord's first portion is twenty-five thousand long and twenty-five thousand broad. Ezekiel 45 also sets apart a holy first portion with that length but gives a breadth of twenty thousand; the differing stated extents remain visible.

Ezekiel 48:10
From these will be the first portion of the holy areas for the priests: twenty-five thousand toward the north, a breadth of ten thousand toward the sea, a breadth of ten thousand toward the east, and a length of twenty-five thousand toward the south. The mountain of the holy places will be in its midst,
  Ezekiel 45:3-4 — repeated priestly sacred plot and sanctuary provision: The priests' holy portion measures twenty-five thousand by ten thousand, with the mountain of the holy places in its midst. Ezekiel 45 likewise gives a plot with those dimensions containing the sanctuary and then assigns the land to ministering priests, additionally mentioning their houses.

Ezekiel 48:11
for the consecrated priests, the sons of Zadok, who keep the duties of the house and did not stray when the sons of Israel strayed, as the Levites strayed.
  Ezekiel 44:15-16 — repeated fidelity of Zadok's sons during Israel's straying: The consecrated sons of Zadok kept the house's duties when Israel and the Levites strayed. Ezekiel 44 likewise contrasts the Zadokite priests' fidelity during Israel's straying and grants them approach and service in God's holy places, explaining their distinct priestly standing.

Ezekiel 48:12
They will have the first portion given from the land’s first portions, most holy, from the Levites’ boundaries.
  Leviticus 27:20-21 — related holy land becoming a priestly possession: The priests receive a most-holy first portion from the land's first portions, from the Levites' boundaries. Leviticus likewise describes a consecrated field becoming holy to the Lord and the priest's possession after release, but through a different consecration-and-redemption procedure rather than Ezekiel's territorial plan.

Ezekiel 48:13
The Levites will have the land adjoining the priests’ boundaries, twenty-five thousand long and ten thousand broad. The whole length will be twenty-five thousand and the breadth twenty thousand.
  Ezekiel 45:5 — repeated Levite allotment dimensions with an additional total: The Levites' adjoining plot is stated as twenty-five thousand by ten thousand, followed by a whole breadth of twenty thousand. Ezekiel 45 likewise gives the Levites twenty-five thousand by ten thousand with dwelling cities; the later additional breadth clause is not flattened into the earlier measurement.

Ezekiel 48:14
None of it will be sold or measured out, nor will the land’s first produce be taken away, for it is holy to the Lord.
  Leviticus 25:32-34 — parallel protection of Levitical land from sale: The land adjoining the priests' allotment may not be sold or measured out, and its first produce may not be removed because it is holy to the Lord. Leviticus likewise protects the fields set apart for Levite cities from sale as their everlasting possession, without repeating Ezekiel's first-produce clause.

Ezekiel 48:15
The five thousand remaining in breadth alongside the twenty-five thousand will be a forewall for the city, for dwelling and its intervening space. The city will be in its midst.
  Ezekiel 45:6 — repeated city allocation of five thousand beside twenty-five thousand: The remaining five thousand in breadth alongside twenty-five thousand serves the city, dwelling and intervening space. Ezekiel 45 likewise gives the city a possession five thousand broad and twenty-five thousand long for the whole house of Israel, while this unit places the city in its midst.

Ezekiel 48:16
These are its measurements: on the north, four thousand five hundred; on the south, four thousand five hundred; on the east, four thousand five hundred; and toward the sea, four thousand five hundred.
  Ezekiel 48:35 — same-chapter arithmetic relation between city sides and circuit: Each of the city's four sides measures four thousand five hundred. Their sum is eighteen thousand, matching the circuit stated in verse 35; this numerical relation supplies no interpretation of the later unit's unusual city-name clause.

Ezekiel 48:17
The city will have an intervening space: two hundred fifty toward the north, two hundred fifty toward the south, two hundred fifty toward the east, and two hundred fifty toward the sea.
  Ezekiel 48:15-16 — same-chapter relation of city width and surrounding intervals: The two hundred fifty intervals on each opposing side, added to the city's four-thousand-five-hundred width, total five thousand. That matches the remaining breadth assigned to the city and intervening space in verse 15, an arithmetic relation within this particular city plan.

Ezekiel 48:18
The remaining length adjoining the first portions of the holy areas will be ten thousand toward the east and ten thousand toward the sea; these will be the holy place’s first portions, and its produce will be bread for the city’s workers.
  Ezekiel 48:19 — same-chapter identity of workers supplied by the allocated produce: The remaining land's produce will be bread for the city's workers. Verse 19 identifies those workers as drawn from all Israel's tribes and says they will work it, linking the specified produce to the people cultivating the allocated land.

Ezekiel 48:19
The city’s workers, from all Israel’s tribes, will work it.
  Ezekiel 45:6 — related civic provision involving all Israel's tribes: The city's workers come from all Israel's tribes and work the assigned land. The earlier city allocation is likewise for the whole house of Israel, connecting the civic provision to Israel broadly without asserting that every Israelite personally works the land.

Ezekiel 48:20
The entire first portion will be twenty-five thousand by twenty-five thousand. You will set apart its holy portion as a square, from the city’s possession.
  Ezekiel 48:9 — same-chapter repeated square extent of the first portion: The entire first portion is twenty-five thousand by twenty-five thousand and is explicitly described as a square. Verse 9 had stated the same length and breadth for the first portion set apart for the Lord; this unit adds its relation to the city's possession.

Ezekiel 48:21
The remainder will belong to the ruler on this side and that side, from the holy first portions and toward the city’s possession, extending twenty-five thousand in length to the eastern boundaries, and toward the sea extending twenty-five thousand to the sea-facing boundaries, adjoining the ruler’s portions. The first portion of the holy areas and the house’s sanctuary will be in its midst.
  Ezekiel 45:7 — repeated royal portions beside the holy and civic allocations: The ruler receives the remainder on either side of the holy first portions and the city's possession, toward the east and sea. Ezekiel 45 likewise positions the ruler's land on the two sides of those same allocations, before this later specification of their extent.

Ezekiel 48:22
From the Levites’ possession and from the city’s possession, it will be in the midst of the rulers; between Judah’s boundaries and Benjamin’s boundaries it will belong to the rulers.
  Ezekiel 45:7-8 — related royal land adjoining the city and holy allocations: The unit relates the Levites' and city's possessions to land belonging to the rulers between Judah and Benjamin. Ezekiel 45 likewise assigns the ruler land beside the holy first portions and city, but does not name these two tribal boundaries in that passage.

Ezekiel 48:23
As for the remaining tribes, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Benjamin, one portion.
  Joshua 18:11 — related territorial allotment for Benjamin: Benjamin receives one east-to-sea portion among the remaining tribes. Joshua likewise assigns Benjamin a lot, there placing its borders between Judah and the sons of Joseph; the reference compares the named tribal allotment without identifying the two boundary arrangements.

Ezekiel 48:24
From Benjamin’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Simeon, one portion.
  Joshua 19:1-9 — contrasting descriptions of Simeon's allotment: Simeon receives a portion from Benjamin's boundaries in Ezekiel's sequence. Joshua instead places Simeon's inheritance amid Judah's lot and identifies its towns, preserving the different descriptions of that tribe's territorial placement.

Ezekiel 48:25
From Simeon’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Issachar, one portion.
  Joshua 19:17-23 — related territorial allotment for Issachar: Issachar receives a portion from Simeon's boundaries. Joshua likewise gives Issachar an inheritance, there the fourth lot with named towns and a boundary ending at the Jordan; it does not repeat Ezekiel's uniform east-to-sea arrangement.

Ezekiel 48:26
From Issachar’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Zebulun, one portion.
  Joshua 19:10-16 — related territorial allotment for Zebulun: Zebulun receives a portion from Issachar's boundaries. Joshua likewise describes Zebulun's inheritance, there the third lot with named borders, cities and villages, rather than Ezekiel's sequence of neighboring tribal portions.

Ezekiel 48:27
From Zebulun’s boundaries, from the eastern parts to the sea-facing parts—Gad, one portion.
  Joshua 13:24-28 — contrasting descriptions of Gad's landed inheritance: Gad receives the next portion from Zebulun's boundaries, extending from the eastern to sea-facing parts. Joshua instead recounts Gad's inheritance from Moses with cities of Gilead and a Jordan boundary extending toward the Sea of Chinnereth, preserving the different territorial descriptions.

Ezekiel 48:28
From Gad’s boundaries toward the southwest, its boundaries will run from Thaiman and the water of Marimoth Kades, an inheritance as far as the Great Sea.
  Ezekiel 47:19 — repeated southwestern boundary through Thaiman and Marimoth Kades: Gad's southwest boundary runs from Thaiman and the water of Marimoth Kades to the Great Sea. The preceding land-boundary account uses those same landmarks on its south and southwest side, additionally naming Palm Grove there.

Ezekiel 48:29
This is the land you will cast lots for among Israel’s tribes, and these are their divisions, says the Lord God.
  Numbers 33:54 — parallel allocation by lots among Israel's tribes: The closing declaration identifies the land to be allotted among Israel's tribes and their divisions. Numbers likewise commands inheritance by lot according to tribes and ancestral houses, there adding a population-based adjustment that this closing Ezekiel unit does not repeat.

Ezekiel 48:31
The city’s gates will bear the names of Israel’s tribes. Three gates will face north: one gate of Reuben, one gate of Judah, and one gate of Levi.
  Exodus 28:21 — related tribal names assigned to city gates and priestly stones: The city gates bear Israel's tribal names, with Reuben, Judah and Levi named on the north. Exodus likewise assigns the twelve tribal names to the oracle-piece's stones, comparing representation through tribal names rather than equating the objects, positions or engraving methods.

Ezekiel 48:35
The circuit will be eighteen thousand. And the city’s name, from whatever day it comes into being, will be its name.”
  Ezekiel 48:16 — same-chapter arithmetic relation between circuit and four city sides: The stated circuit of eighteen thousand equals the four sides of four thousand five hundred given in verse 16. This supports the measured-city connection only: the supplied city-name clause is preserved and is not replaced by the traditional name The Lord is there.
